Teacher evidence only: the matched companion guide of the Grade 3 student book cited in this lesson — general guidance (including the Fase B ATP table) plus one guide section per student chapter, with teaching guidance, misconception notes and answer keys; its Bab 1 answer-key tables (printed pp. 64–91) key every practice item, not only the chapter tests (printed pp. 1–230). It teaches no pupil content beyond the student book. Guide citation stocked by the 2026-11-08 re-verification sweep.

**Book 183:** *Buku Panduan Guru Matematika untuk SD/MI Kelas V* (teacher guide) — Indonesia, Meita Fitrianawati, Ika Surtiani, Ait Istiandaru, Kementerian Pendidikan, Kebudayaan, Riset, dan Teknologi — Pusat Perbukuan (Kurikulum Merdeka), 2022, ISBN 978-602-427-899-1. Filed in maths/books/indonesia.md (FULLY READ + CITATION CHECK PASSED). Teacher evidence only: the matched companion guide of the Grade 5 student book cited in this lesson — Panduan Umum (printed pp. 1–14, stating the Fase C outcomes, which cover more than this Grade 5 book alone) plus one guide section per student chapter, with full answer keys (a 0–4 scoring rubric per test item), misconception notes, differentiation, remedial and enrichment tasks, parent interaction and reading lists (printed pp. 1–428). It teaches no pupil content beyond the student book. Guide citation stocked by the 2026-11-08 re-verification sweep.

| Right angles by folding — angles and shapes: make a right angle (Тэгш өнцөг) by folding a circular paper twice, draw around the folded shape on squared paper; the right angle is marked with a small square sign; check whether shapes have right angles by laying the folded corner on them: a triangle whose sides coincide is right-angled; test a rectangle, a triangle, a cut-corner pentagon and a parallelogram | Mongolia, Математик II (Grade 2), printed p. 24 |
| Angle, vertex, sides — an angle (Өнцөг): trace a folded triangular paper's corner — the region bounded by two sides (тал) from one vertex (орой) is an angle; angle sizes compared by how far the sides open (angle 1 against angle 2); build right-angled shapes on a geoboard; check the marked angles on six shapes are right angles; fold an irregular paper to make a right angle; draw right-angled shapes with a ruler | Mongolia, Математик II (Grade 2), printed p. 25 |
| Recognising shapes (Дүрсийг таних): pictures show a window corner, a swing's frame and opening fans: what do you know about angles (өнцөг)? Find examples of angles from the pictures at the top of the page; draw angles using a ruler and a pencil (three constructions shown: mark a point in your notebook; from the point draw a straight line (шулуун) — a ray is formed; then draw another ray from the same point — an angle is formed); the angle's parts are named: side (тал) and vertex (орой); make right angles using paper, a pencil case and plasticine (a make-and-do task: a. fold the paper as in the picture and make a right angle (the two folded edges meeting fully make a right angle); b. make a right angle with a pencil case (savkh); c. make a right angle with plasticine); a right angle made by folding paper is used to check: check which of the pictured angles are bigger than a right angle, which are smaller, and which are equal to it (four angles a-d drawn with different openings) | Mongolia, Математик III (Grade 3), printed p. 31 |
| Acute, obtuse and straight angles — the definitions are given in a box: an angle whose size is bigger than a right angle is called an obtuse angle (мохоо өнцөг); an angle whose size is smaller than a right angle is called an acute angle (хурц өнцөг); an angle formed by two right angles with one side coinciding (laid out flat) is called a straight angle (дэлгэмэл өнцөг); draw a right angle (shown with a set square on squared paper: the right angle is marked as in the picture); draw acute and obtuse angles and explain how you drew them; draw the table in your notebook (columns: contains a right angle; contains an acute angle; contains an obtuse angle) and find examples of angles from the pictures (a clock face, a window, a pedestrian-crossing sign, an octagonal sweet box, ice-cream cones) — classify them into the table by which angle they contain and explain what you did; what sum value comes out of the numbers in the table? First round each number and then add: a. round each number by tens (23, 45, 37, 55; 61, 52, 39, 18; 77, 65, 33, 15); b. round each number by hundreds (693, 328, 754; 286, 819, 147; 571, 465, 932); c. round each number by thousands (2065, 610, 172; 726, 1148, 1504; 1689, 590, 213) — calculate and check (each table's rows, columns and diagonals give the same sum — a magic square of rounded numbers); when checking, draw the table in your notebook and write the numbers that come out when rounded; a self-check game: name the angles — the players take turns forming an angle by moving the clock's two hands; the other player names the angle; check whether the guess was right | Mongolia, Математик III (Grade 3), printed p. 32 |
| Test and consolidation: angles and lines — a test (Сорих): draw the following angles in your notebook: a. a right angle; b. an obtuse angle; c. an acute angle; d. a straight angle (дэлгэмэл); draw two polygons: write the names and the properties of the shapes you drew; classify the shapes and write their numbers in the table (a right triangle, a hexagon, a quadrilateral, a pentagon, a chevron shape; columns: has parallel sides; has mutually perpendicular sides; has a right angle); draw the axes of symmetry of the symmetric shapes; consolidation (Бататгах): do the paper folding and answer: a. into how many acute and obtuse angles is a straight angle divided (two crossed lines with coloured angle regions)? b. into how many right and acute angles is an obtuse angle divided?; find mutually perpendicular and parallel lines: in the picture, colour the angles by colour: find right, acute, obtuse and straight angles (two diagrams of intersecting lines); copy the given segment into your notebook: draw a segment perpendicular to it and a segment parallel to it, separately (four given segments: vertical, horizontal, diagonal, diagonal); how many right angles are there (a notebook, a set square (шугам), a room corner, a box — count the right angles in each pictured object)? | Mongolia, Математик III (Grade 3), printed p. 39 |
| Consolidation: angles, symmetry, perimeter — recognising and distinguishing shapes and solids: classify the angles and write their numbers in the table (five angle diagrams are numbered 1-5; the table's columns: bigger than a right angle; a right angle; smaller than a right angle): how will you check?; in which direction does the clock hand turn? Explain what you understand by "turning in the clock hand's direction" and "turning against the clock hand's direction" (a clock face is pictured); join 2 paper strips together: rotating them in which clock direction was an angle formed? Determine how many right angles the angles formed along the red and the green arrows are (four diagrams of joined strips turned by different amounts: a quarter turn, a half turn, a three-quarter turn and a full turn); find the right angle: how did you know (three angle pictures: an acute angle, a right angle and an obtuse angle)?; in the writing of which letters and digits do right, acute and obtuse angles appear?; a self-check activity: make angles using a disc shape: prepare two discs of the same measure and different colours, and scissors: a. cut both discs along a radius; b. slot the cut discs into each other; c. rotate one disc and make various angles: which of them is bigger than a right angle, and which smaller?; draw the shape in your notebook in imitation: continue it on by symmetry (two grid pictures: a coloured stepped shape beside a dashed axis to be completed, and a green staircase triangle pattern under a diagonal dashed line); make a pattern by continuing the shape (a blue tile pattern at the left of a long empty grid); for which shape is the symmetry axis drawn correctly? Why? For the other shapes, why do you say it was drawn wrongly (four grid shapes with dashed lines: an irregular octagon with a vertical line; a rectangle with a diagonal line; a square with an off-centre horizontal line; a house-shaped pentagon with a vertical line through its middle)?; a rectangle-shaped vegetable field is 45 m long and 8 m wide: Bayar walked once around the field looking at the vegetable crop: find the length of the road he walked (106 m): if he goes round 2 times, what road will he travel (212 m)?; a self-check: 1) a square-shaped running track is 10 m long: if you run round it 6 times, what road will you travel (240 m)?; 2) make shapes: one player makes various shapes on a geoboard (гео самбар): the other player names the shape and tells its property and a feature they know: the first pupil says whether it is right: the players swap roles and play; 3) find the pictured shape's axis of symmetry and draw it (an isosceles trapezoid is pictured); write the names of the shapes and solids and distinguish them in your notebook (the table: a quadrilateral / not a quadrilateral; the pictured items: a green many-sided polygon, a pink rectangle, a red hexagon, a circle, a blue pentagon, a yellow circle, a grey triangle and a drawn cube): depict them distinguished by one other property and by two properties; Tuya showed flat shapes in the picture on her right hand, 6 of each: a. using how many of which shape can a cube be made? Explain your answer (the pictures: a rectangle 2 cm long and 1 cm wide, and a square of side 1 cm — six 1 cm squares make the cube's net); b. find the area of the surface of the cube that will be made (6 cm.kv); draw a circle of diameter 4 cm with one centre and a disc of radius 1 cm: mark the three points lying at distances of 1 cm, 2 cm and 3 cm from the disc's centre and write letters by them: write where the points lie in relation to the circle and the disc; draw a segment 14 cm long: draw a segment whose length is equal to four sevenths of it (8 cm): what is the difference of the two segments' lengths (6 cm)? Compose a similar problem yourselves and solve it; draw a square whose perimeter is 16 cm: which rectangles have the same perimeter as it? Find the area of the square you drew and of each rectangle; an activity: the game "What I know about shapes and solids": prepare drawing-notebook paper, scissors, coloured and ordinary pencils and a pen: play in groups and in pairs: a. cut the drawing-notebook paper in halves several times; b. on one small paper draw one shape or solid; c. on the other papers write one property each of the shape or solid; d. mix the papers and put them face down: draw one out and talk about the shape or solid chosen | Mongolia, Математик III (Grade 3), printed pp. 141–143 |
| Measuring angles (Өнцөг хэмжих): a. find the things around you that form angles and discuss what angle is formed; b. make an angle with a book, a pen, a pencil and a ruler, and discuss (the classroom picture: the set square on the board, the open door's angle marked in orange); the box: "The figure formed by two rays starting from one point is called an angle (өнцөг). It is denoted as angle A or ∠BAC" (the diagram of the angle with vertex A and sides to B and C, shaded yellow); fill in which of the words acute (хурц), obtuse (мохоо) and right (тэгш) fits in place of the dots (the angle formed between mutually perpendicular lines, shown by the set square, is a …… angle (right); an angle smaller than a right angle is a …… angle (acute); an angle bigger than a right angle is a …… angle (obtuse); the ordering strip: acute angle < right angle < obtuse angle); denote the angles with capital letters and match each with the suitable word (the five pictured angles, to be matched with right, acute, obtuse, straight (дэлгэмэл) and acute); the box: an angle's degree measure is measured with a protractor (транспортир): the protractor's scale runs from 0° to 180° (the pictured protractor with its centre (төв) and scale (хуваарь) labelled); let's find an angle's degree measure using the protractor (the four steps: 1. place the protractor's centre so it coincides with the angle's vertex A; 2. place the protractor so its 0° scale coincides with the angle's side AB; 3. look at which scale of the protractor the side AC coincides with; 4. this scale determines the degree measure of the angle ∠BAC); name the angles, look at the protractor's measure and write it (the example: ∠B = 75°, or ∠ABC = 75°; the two pictured protractor readings); name the angles and find their measures (the three lettered angles); denote the angle's vertex with a capital letter and check the measure (the four angles printed with their measures 45°, 23°, 72° and 120°); name the angles and measure them with the protractor: write which of the angles is acute, obtuse, right or straight (the example: ∠AOB = 85°, an acute angle; the pictured right angle and the narrow angle); measure the following angles and then find their sum, and discuss (knowing the measure of the angle ∠AOC, can the measure of the angle ∠COB be found without using a protractor? Experiment and draw a conclusion — the adjacent angles on the straight line AOB sum to 180°); measure the angles formed between two lines and discuss the result (a. which of the angles among them are equal to each other (the vertical angles where the lines AB and CD cross at O); b. knowing one angle's measure, can the other 3 angles' measures be found without a protractor? Experiment and explain); an activity: let's learn to estimate an angle's measure (Өнцгийн хэмжээг баримжаалж сурцгаая): two players play together: the first player draws any angle and says approximately how many degrees this angle's measure is; then the first player measures the drawn angle with the protractor: check whether the angle's measure was estimated correctly and give a score using the table (an error (Алдаа) of 0°-5° scores 10 points (Оноо), 6°-10° scores 5, 11°-15° scores 2): the player with the most points wins; building parallel and perpendicular lines (Параллел, перпендикуляр шулуун байгуулах): a. find parallel and mutually perpendicular lines in the picture and discuss them (the ger (yurt) encampment scene with its fence, cart and animals); b. find parallel and perpendicular lines among the things around you and name them (the definitions pictured: lines that form a right angle where they cross are called perpendicular lines (перпендикуляр шулуун); lines that never cross each other are called parallel lines (параллел шулуун)); build a perpendicular line (a. draw a line; b. mark a point A lying outside the line; c. draw through the point A a vertical line forming a right angle, using the set square); build a parallel line (a. draw a line; b. mark two points A and B at the same distance from the line, using the set square in its two positions; c. draw the line passing through the points A and B) | Mongolia, Математик IV (Grade 4), printed pp. 84–87 |

**Acceptance count: taught in 72 countries (Kazakhstan, Jordan, South Africa, Egypt, Greece, Armenia, Sierra Leone, Mauritania, Libya, Vietnam, Ghana, Laos, Yemen, Indonesia, Nicaragua, Ethiopia, South Sudan, Iraq, Bangladesh, Nepal, Cambodia, Kuwait, Iran, China, Pakistan, Syria, Saudi Arabia, Malaysia, Rwanda, Sudan, India, Mongolia, Venezuela, North Macedonia, Afghanistan, Uzbekistan, Ecuador, Albania, Bolivia, Tajikistan, Palestine, Turkey, Tanzania, El Salvador, Cyprus, Sri Lanka, Colombia, Angola, Moldova, Australia, Brazil, Bhutan, Cabo Verde, Costa Rica, Cuba, Bulgaria, Eritrea, Chile, Finland, Dominica, Dominican Republic, Guyana, Gabon, Ireland, Fiji, Honduras, Italy, Iceland, Mozambique, Nigeria, Papua New Guinea, Timor-Leste).** Egypt carries the fullest single Iran measures angles with a protractor in Grade 4, bisects angles and sums polygon angles in Grade 5, and gives the (n − 2) × 180° interior-angle rule in Grade 8.
treatment — the angle kinds, the protractor, complementary and
supplementary pairs, the transversal's eight angles, and the
triangle and quadrilateral sums in one Grade 7 unit. Jordan
carries the climb from naming angles in Grade 3, through
measuring and drawing them in Grade 4 and the straight-line and
round-a-point sums in Grade 5, to the full relationships unit in
Grade 7. Sierra Leone carries the same climb in JSS 1 with the
paper-protractor trick and the corner/straight memory words for
complementary and supplementary. Libya carries first protractor
work in Grade 4. Vietnam carries measuring angles and the
acute/obtuse/straight kinds in Grade 4. Greece carries the kinds
and the triangle/quadrilateral sums in Grade 6. Armenia carries
measuring angles in Grade 5 and the triangle angle sum in
Geometry 7. Kazakhstan carries the kinds in Grade 2.
Mauritania carries angle recognition in Grade 3 and angles in
Grade 6. South Africa carries interior angles in Grade 9.
Ghana's evidence is teacher-manual only (JICA B5 Lessons
101–102): no pupil textbook in Ghana's set teaches angles, and
the lesson words Ghana that way throughout. Laos carries angles from identifying them in Grade 3, through protractor measurement in Grade 4, to kinds, vertical angles and triangle and quadrilateral angle sums in Grades 6–7 and inscribed and central angles in Grade 8. Yemen carries angles from the angle defined in Grade 3, through angle types and measuring angles, to alternate, corresponding and co-interior angles and the triangle angle sum. Indonesia carries angles from what an angle is and the five angle types in Grade 3, through the full angle kinds with protractor work in Grade 5, to vertical, corresponding, alternate and same-side pairs with complementary and supplementary angles in Grade 7 — with the Grade 7 student book's Table 5.2 error corrected: its same-side exterior pairs are wrong (p. 167) and the guide's correct pairs (α and η, β and θ, p. 191) are taught here. Nicaragua carries angles from the right angle in Grade 2 and the lines-and-angles unit in Grade 3, through degree measurement with a protractor and the triangle and quadrilateral angle sums in Grade 4, to angle relations and the transversal in Grades 7–8 (student book and module), inscribed angles in Grade 9, and degree/minute/second and radian measure in Grade 10 (student book and module, the module adding the centesimal system). Ethiopia carries angles from naming types only in Grade 4 (no degrees there), through measuring with a protractor and drawing angles of given measures in Grade 5, to adjacent and vertical angles, complementary and supplementary pairs and the transversal in Grade 6, angles revised in Grade 7, complementary and supplementary angles and exterior angles in Grade 8, and the interior and exterior angles of polygons in Grade 9. South Sudan carries angles from types and protractor use in Grade 4, through constructing 60°/30°/90°/45° in Grade 5 and bisecting lines and angles with vertically opposite and supplementary angles in Grade 6, to transversals in Grade 7, angles and triangle and polygon angle sums in Secondary 1, and angles read off the unit circle in Secondary 2 — with the Primary 6 line-bisection section cited as angle bisection (its steps bisect an angle) and the Primary 7 book's claim that all transversal angles are congruent corrected: only corresponding and alternate pairs (with parallel lines) are congruent. Iraq carries angles from naming them in Grade 3, through the straight angle and measuring and drawing with a protractor in Grades 4–5 and the triangle and quadrilateral angle sums, to complementary and supplementary angles and angle bisection in Grade 6, polygon angles in Grade 7, angle/line theorems in Grade 8, angles and the circle in Grade 9, and the directed angle in Grade 10 Scientific. Bangladesh carries angles from the acute/right/obtuse/straight kinds in Grade 3, through degrees, the protractor, complementary and supplementary pairs and vertically opposite angles in Grade 4, the relation among a triangle's angles in Grade 7 and the quadrilateral angle sum in Grade 8, to positive and negative angles, quadrants, coterminal angles and radian measure in Grade 9. Nepal carries angles from the angle as the 'splitness' between clock hands in Grade 3, through measuring angles with a protractor and constructing them in steps of 10° in Grade 4, to lines and angles with bisectors and constructions in Grade 6, angle constructions with adjacent, vertically opposite, complementary and supplementary angles and the transversal in Grade 7, and adjacent, vertically opposite and transversal angles in Grade 8. Cambodia carries angles whole from what an angle is in Grade 4 through angle types, bisectors, constructions and parallel lines in Grades 5–8, with the angle between lines in Grade 10 and directed angles in Grade 11. Kuwait carries angles from lines and angles in Grade 4, through basic geometry in Grade 5 and the Grade 6 geometry unit, to angles and parallel lines in Grade 7 and the parallel-line angle tests with proofs in Grade 8. North Macedonia enters from its main set — Grade 2 (2023), Grade 3 (2023), Grade 4 (2023), Grade 5 (2022), Grade 6 (2023), Grade 7 (2025) and Grade 11 (2026).

China carries angles from Grade 4 triangle classification and angle sums, through Grade 7 parallel-line geometry, to the senior general angle and radian measure — with the space angles (skew lines, line–plane, dihedral) carried in both senior editions.

Pakistan (Punjab) carries angles from Grade 4's protractor work through Class 6's transversal and polygon angles to Class 9's bisector and side–angle theorems.

Syria meets the angle in Grade 3 beside point, line, ray and segment, names and classifies angles in Grade 4, measures with the protractor in Grade 5, and proves the working facts in Grades 6–7 — vertically opposite angles, the straight-angle sum, complementary and supplementary pairs, the transversal equalities with their converses, and the triangle's 180° found by folding a cut-out triangle.

Saudi Arabia introduces the angle in Grade 4 — degrees alongside turns, the right angle named at 90°, acute and obtuse compared by size, and angles drawn to a given measure.

Malaysia builds angles in Form 1 from measuring to parallels and transversals, then closes the polygon angle sums in Form 2, deriving the interior sum by triangulating from one vertex.

Rwanda teaches angles from Year 2, where right, acute and obtuse angles are compared without degrees, through Year 3's protractor work in both editions, Year 4's complementary and supplementary angles, Year 5's transversal angles, Year 6's polygon angle sums, and Senior 1's lines-and-angles unit.

Sudan teaches angles from Grade 4 (the angle, measuring and drawing with the protractor, types, perpendicular and parallel lines), the Grade 5 angle relations (complementary, supplementary, vertically opposite, transversal and parallel-line angles, the triangle's angle sum and exterior angle), interior and exterior sums in Grade 7, and in Grade 8 the side–angle theorems of the triangle — the greater side opposite the greater angle and its converse, the perpendicular as the shortest distance to a line, and the triangle inequality itself, formally proved with its corollary — the very parts Pakistan's Class 9 book carries in this lesson.

India carries angles through its NCERT ladder — Math-Magic Class 5 and Mathematics Class 6–7, 9 — including what an angle is; naming angles; complementary angles: two angles summing to 90°; kinds of angles.


Mongolia enters through its ministry textbooks Математик II, III, IV, V, VI, VII, VIII (Grades 2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 7, 8): right angles by folding; angle, vertex, sides; recognising angles; the right angle.

Venezuela enters through the Colección Bicentenario, the MPPE's one-book-per-grade course: *Aventuras de Patacalientes* (Grade 3), *Contando con los recursos* (Grade 4), *La Patria Buena* (Grade 5), *Matemática Para La Vida* (Grade 7) and *Conciencia Matemática* (Grade 8). From its books: from Grade 3: what an angle is — el ángulo formed by two sides (lados) meeting at a vértice; the right angle (ángulo recto) recognised; from Grade 5: angle sum — The triangle's interior angles sum to 180°, discovered by cutting the corners off and laying them on a straight line; from Grade 8: puzzles — clock-hand angles at 12:15 and at 3:10; dividing a clock face into three equal-sum parts.

Afghanistan enters through its Ministry of Education course — the Grade 1 course in two language editions (Pashto and Dari, one work with identical page maps) and Dari student books for Grades 4, 6, 7, 8, 10, 11 and 12, all printing year 1398 HS except the Grade 8 book, which prints 1399 HS on its cover and title page and 1398 HS on its specifications page. From its books: from Grade 6: the protractor: measuring and drawing angles; drawing an angle with a protractor; from Grade 7: classification of triangles by their angles; sum of the interior angles of a triangle; exterior angles of a triangle; sum of the interior angles of a polygon; sum of the exterior angles of a polygon; alternate interior and exterior angles; corresponding angles; consecutive interior angles on the same side of a transversal (supplementary); exterior angles of a quadrilateral (sum 360°).

Albania enters through its Mediaprint teacher's books (Libër Mësuesi, in Albanian) — TEACHER EVIDENCE only: no pupil's book is in hand for any grade, so Albania is never cited as a pupil-textbook source; what the teacher's books evidence is what is taught and in what order, through their lesson plans, worked answers and answer keys. From its books: from Grade 5 (teacher's book): The angle; angles in geometric figures; types of angles; Angles in a triangle (sum found by measuring/activity). from Grade 6 (teacher's book): : the angle and its types; the triangle and its types (plans 64–65). from Grade 8 (teacher's book): .2 Alternate (corresponding/vertically opposite) angles and proofs (plan 57); .4 Interior and exterior angles of polygons (plan 59); .5 Solving geometry problems (plan 60). from Grade 9 (teacher's book): Corresponding and alternate angles on parallel lines; angle sum in polygons; measuring angles with a…; Missing angles and sides in triangles; interior angle sum 180°; isosceles/equilateral properties; altitude…. from Grade 10 (teacher's book): Angle facts in triangles and quadrilaterals; worked exterior-angle chain (118° ⇒ 62° ⇒ 28°) verified; Interior angle sum 180(n − 2); regular polygon angle (pentagon 108° ⇒ n = 5 worked; see error note on the…; Exterior angles of polygons sum to 360°.

Bolivia enters through the Área Matemática sections of its Ministerio de Educación (Estado Plurinacional de Bolivia) Textos de Aprendizaje — the student books for Grades 7, 8, 10 and 12, each the maths section of that year's integrated year book, keeping the whole book's printed foliation, with no imprint or ISBN printed in any of them — and through the Compendio para Maestras y Maestros 2023–2024, the ministry's teacher compilation of the official student texts for all six Secundaria years (Grades 7–12), cited as teacher-compilation evidence only, never as or in place of a pupil text; its Secundaria 3 and Secundaria 5 sections are the only in-hand source for Grades 9 and 11, whose student texts are not in the record. From its books: from Grade 7: Definición de ángulo; Operaciones básicas con ángulos; Clasificación de ángulos; Ángulos complementarios y suplementarios; from Grade 8: Ecuaciones en geometría; Suma de ángulos internos; Ángulos externos; from the Compendium's Secundaria 2 section (Grade 8): Introducción a la trigonometría; from the Compendium's Secundaria 5 section (Grade 11): Trigonometría y la aplicación en la tecnología.

Tajikistan enters through its Ministry-approved pupil's textbooks in Tajik (Cyrillic), all published in Dushanbe — Математика for Grade 5 (Маориф, 2022) and Grade 6 (5th edition, Маориф, 2021), Алгебра for Grades 7 (4th improved edition, 2023) and 8 (4th edition, 2023), and Геометрия — the continuation of stereometry — for Grade 11 (3rd edition, Лавҳ, 2020). From its books: from Grade 5: Angle: vertex and sides, naming with three letters; Straight, right, acute and obtuse angles; Measuring angles: the degree as 1/180 of a straight angle, protractor; Measuring angles with a protractor (review, Chapter 9).

Palestine enters through the pupil's books of its Ministry of Education (Curriculum Centre, Ramallah), all in Arabic — Mathematics Grade 1 in two parts (both fourth edition, 2020), Grade 10 in two parts (Part One third edition, 2020; Part Two second edition, 2019 — the held parts are different editions and are cited as such), and the Grade 12 Technological-stream book (التكنولوجي, first edition, 2019 — the book's own name for its stream, never "Information Technology"). No ISBN is printed in any of the five books. From its books: from Grade 10 Part Two: directed angles and standard position; quadrants; quadrantal angles.

Barbados enters as CURRICULUM EVIDENCE only, through the *Primary Mathematics Syllabus* (Classes 1–4) of its Ministry of Education and Human Resource Development — its Class 3 syllabus requires the types of angles — acute, obtuse, right and straight, with naming and drawing angles — and whole, half and quarter turns expressed in degrees in the body; Class 4 requires the types of angles including reflex, and comparing angles, while measurement of angles is named in its scope and sequence only, with no measurement objective in the Class 4 targets or body. A syllabus states what is to be taught, in what order and to what depth; it teaches nothing itself. Barbados publishes no government pupil textbooks — primary mathematics books are commercial titles parents buy, and the syllabi's Suggested Texts appendices name them (*Caribbean Primary Mathematics* Levels 1–6; *Nelson Primary Maths for Caribbean Schools* 1–4; *Steps To Common Entrance Mathematics* 1–3) as named by the syllabus only: none is in hand and none is cited. Barbados is therefore named in this lesson's countries line and its syllabus requirements are cited in the table as curriculum evidence, but Barbados is not counted in the taught-in figure.

Turkey enters through the pupil's textbooks of its Millî Eğitim Bakanlığı (MEB — Ministry of National Education), all in Turkish, first volumes (1. Kitap) only — Grade 3 (ISBN 978-975-11-9897-6, TTKB approval 13/07/2026), Grade 6 (ISBN 978-975-11-8773-4, TTKB approval 30.06.2025) and Grade 7 (ISBN 978-975-11-9899-0, TTKB approval 1.07.2026); no edition, printing or year line is printed in any of the three books, and the grades' second volumes (2. Kitap) are not in hand and are cited nowhere. From its books: from Grade 7: Angle bisector defined: a line, ray or segment dividing an angle into two equal angles; an angle's symmetry axis is its bisector.

Tanzania enters through the pupil's book of its Tanzania Institute of Education (TIE) — *Mathematics Standard Five — Pupil's Book*, the English-medium Standard Five book (© TIE 2019, published 2019, reprinted 2021; ISBN 978-9987-09-109-6; Certificate of Approval No. 282, approved by the Ministry of Education, Science and Technology on 5 December 2019, prepared to the 2016 primary Mathematics Syllabus). It is the only Tanzania mathematics book in hand: the other 20 recorded works — among them the Swahili-medium Hisabati Std 5, a separate book from this English one — are waiting-for-file and are cited nowhere. From its book: from Standard Five: angles formed where two rays meet, the degree as unit, an angle named with the vertex letter in the middle (∠BAC); the types — acute, right with its small-square marker, obtuse, straight, reflex, full; angles constructed by estimation only, with no protractor anywhere in the book.

Armenia's stock in this lesson is widened by the full re-verification sweep of 2026-11-08, which re-read Armenia's maths books page by page and rebuilt the placements from the PDFs. Newly stocked from its Geometry 7; Geometry 10: ray; angle; comparing angles; measuring angles; units of angle measurement; measuring tools; adjacent and vertical angles; classifying angles; perpendicular lines; exterior angle of a triangle; criteria of parallelism of lines; theorems on angles formed by two parallel lines and a transversal; sum of a triangle's angles.

Egypt's stock in this lesson is completed by the same sweep: angles formed by the intersection of two straight lines and a transversal, in Secondary 1 Term 1.

## Morocco added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Morocco's whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs — **32 books, all of them teacher guides or teacher editions (Dar Attakafa, Casablanca); 0 pupil (student) books** — and placed 610 teaching items. No Moroccan pupil maths textbook is in hand: the pupil's books are commercial print, and the library's systematic free shelf is Dar Attakafa's teacher-guide library. Every Morocco item in this section is therefore TEACHER EVIDENCE — a guide states the pupil book's lesson, its content and its order — cited as the evidence kind it is; Morocco is counted in NO lesson's taught-in figure, on the standing Ghana evidence-only precedent, until Moroccan pupil books are in hand and read. Set reconciliation, as the placements prove it: the 32 are distinct works (Le Monde des Maths ×6, L'Explicite en Maths ×6, Al Moufid primary ×3, Al Marjie primary ×2, Al Moufid collège ×3, Switch On! guides ×6, Switch On! Every Day booklets ×6; French 27 books, Arabic 5); textbooks.tsv carries only 28 of the 32 — a library-record gap, reported here and not edited (merges never edit the records). The primary guides all teach the one official revised programme (July 2020) — 32 lessons per grade, Grades 1–6, stated independently by up to four series per grade; the collège guides give the BIOF programme distributions for Grades 7–9. Read depth, as the placements declare it: the Arabic guides' legacy font encoding makes pdftotext return mojibake, so their contents were read from rendered pages; Books 6, 7 and 25 place their own year's programme distribution only (their readings of the other collège years are excluded, so nothing is double-counted); Book 31 places the distinct rows of its yearly programme table, a coarser grain than the lesson-by-lesson guides. The placements' counting grain is one item per distinct lesson/topic the guide teaches the teacher to deliver, and its named exclusions — method/framework chapters, evaluation/bilan/support apparatus and the Switch On confirmation tests, Switch On 1's graphisme weeks, bibliographies, indexes and recording tables — are stocked nowhere. Into this lesson the placements stock 13 teacher-evidence items from 11 of the guides (Book 2, Book 5, Book 10, Book 19, Book 20, Book 22, Book 23, Book 24, Book 27, Book 28, Book 31; Grades 2, 3, 5, 6).

**Book 2:** *Le Monde des Maths 2, Guide de l'enseignant(e), 2e année primaire* (teacher guide, Grade 2, French) — Morocco, Dar Attakafa (Casablanca); 9,555,550 bytes. ## El Salvador added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read El Salvador's whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs — the MINEDUCYT **ESMATE** programme (Proyecto de Mejoramiento de los Aprendizajes en Matemática en Educación Básica y Educación Media, MINEDUCYT–JICA, 2015–2019): **63 filed entries = 43 distinct works** — 16 student files covering the 11 grade textbooks (Grades 1–11; Grade 1 in 4 part-files, Grade 2 in 3), 25 teacher guides, and 2 JICA materials documents (the materials-use guideline and the Japan–El Salvador unit list) filed 11 times each, byte-identical, MD5-proved — the 2 documents are programme apparatus with no teaching content, cited once each in the country record and stocked in no lesson. The read placed 383 teaching items: 281 from the student textbooks and 102 teacher-evidence items from the guides (one per unit per grade). In the sections below the student books are cited as Books S1–S13 and the guides as Books T1–T12, in grade order. El Salvador COUNTS in this lesson's taught-in figure on the strength of its student-textbook rows; its guide rows are TEACHER EVIDENCE — the guides reproduce the student lessons with teaching plans, worked solutions and a unit test per unit — cited as the evidence kind they are and never the basis of the count. Book errors: none that changes a placement (the Grade 9 book's history opener dates al-Khwārizmī to c. 825 BC; he lived c. 780–850 AD — a front-matter error in an excluded motivation essay, in no teaching item, so nothing is taught from it). The placements' named exclusions — *Recortables* cut-out pages, covers, credits and unit-opener history/motivation essays, the GeoGebra practice lessons of Grades 10–11, end-of-unit and end-of-trimester tests in the student books, teacher-guide front matter, and the Grade 6 book's *Repaso* review sections — are stocked nowhere.

Into this lesson the placements stock 7 items (5 from student books, 2 teacher evidence) from Book S6, Book S7, Book T6, Book S10, Book T9.

## Cyprus added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Cyprus's whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs — **75 distinct works: 22 pupil textbooks (primary Grades 1–6, part-books) and 53 teacher guides (Οδηγός Εκπαιδευτικού), all Cyprus Ministry of Education (Υπουργείο Παιδείας), in Greek** — and placed 281 teaching items: 228 from the pupil books and 53 teacher-evidence items (one per guide, worded from the guide's stated success indicators, ΔΕΙΚΤΕΣ ΕΠΙΤΥΧΙΑΣ). SET CORRECTION: the expansion map recorded Cyprus Maths as 22 student + 43 teacher = 65 in hand; the records' union proves 22 student + 53 teacher = **75** (staging books.json carries 64 files = 22 student + 42 teacher, all secured in GitHub; textbooks.tsv adds 11 further teacher guides held as git-blob files that are in no books.json: Grade 1 Units 1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 7, 9, 10, 11, Grade 2 Unit 1, Grade 4 Unit 1) — the correction is recorded here; the library records themselves are not edited (merges never edit the records). In the sections below the pupil books are cited as Books S1–S22 and the guides as Books T1–T53, in grade order. Cyprus COUNTS in this lesson's taught-in figure on the strength of its pupil-book rows; its guide rows are TEACHER EVIDENCE — a guide states the pupil book's unit, its indicators and its lesson organisation — cited as the evidence kind they are and never the basis of the count (the standing Ghana evidence precedent for guide rows). The read's three flagged claims are carried exactly: (1) the map's “65” is corrected above; (2) the Grade 3 Unit 1 teacher guide's indicator Αρ2.1 states quantities “to 10,000” where its pupil book's Unit 1 teaches to 1,000 — the guide's figure is NOT confirmed by the pupil book and is worded as the guide's own claim in that guide's row, never as pupil teaching; (3) three guides carry the books' own unit-number mislabels — the Grade 4 Unit 3 guide's contents header reads “ΕΝΟΤΗΤΑ 2”, the Grade 6 Unit 3 guide's running head reads “ΕΝΟΤΗΤΑ 2”, the Grade 6 Unit 9 guide's running head reads “ΕΝΟΤΗΤΑ 8” — the contents match their cover units, so the rows record the true units with the errors noted in the book entries, taught corrected, never as printed. Production date stamps inside three files (Grade 5 Part 1 “22/06/2026”, Grade 6 Part 1's InDesign stamp 05/03/2026, Grade 6 Part 3 “01/07/2026”) are PDF production stamps in the ministry files as held, not edition dates. The placements' named exclusions — front matter, icon keys, contents pages, cut-out card appendices (“Για τη σελίδα …”), Διαμορφωτική Αξιολόγηση (formative assessment) pages and enrichment sets repeating unit content — are stocked nowhere; the apparent “330C/20C” in the Grade 3 Part 1 enrichment is the degree sign merging with the digits in the text layer (33 °C falling by 2 °C), not a book error. Teacher-guide coverage in hand: Grades 1–3 Units 1–12 complete, Grade 4 Units 1–3 only, Grade 5 Units 1–4, Grade 6 Units 1–10; the further guides that exist only as ministry hub links (Grade 4 Units 4+, Grade 5 Units 5+, Grade 6 Units 11+) are not in hand, not read and not counted. Cyprus's class for Maths is **COURSES-CLOSED** — every in-hand book (75/75) has been read and placed; if the hub-link guides are later secured, Cyprus reverts to PARTIAL until they are read.

Into this lesson the placements stock 5 items (3 from pupil books, 2 teacher evidence) from Book S7, Book S18, Book S21, Book T20, Book T48.

Printed folio ≈ PDF page − 3; pages cited are printed folios as the read cites them.

## Sri Lanka added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Sri Lanka's whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs in two passes — **94 files / 66 distinct works: 26 pupil textbooks and 40 teacher guides, all Sri Lanka Educational Publications Department (EPD) / National Institute of Education (NIE), in the country's three media — English, Sinhala and Tamil** — and placed 479 items: 289 from the pupil books and 190 teacher-evidence items (the secondary guides at one item per term of the guide's own lesson sequence; the primary guides at one item per syllabus competency group or per part). Pass 1 read the expansion map's 59 files (32 works); Pass 2 pinned 35 further in-hand files the map's subject filter had missed — 34 NIE teacher guides for Grades 1–13 and one pupil textbook (Book S26, filed under the subject "Science for Technology") — and read every one. In the sections below the pupil books are cited as Books S1–S26 and the guides as Books T1–T40. Sri Lanka COUNTS in a lesson's taught-in figure only on the strength of its pupil-book rows; its guide rows are TEACHER EVIDENCE — a guide states its syllabus's competencies, its lesson sequence and its teaching instructions — cited as the evidence kind they are and never the basis of the count (the standing Ghana evidence precedent for guide rows). Set facts carried exactly: (1) the Grade 9 Sinhala pupil book is held only as chapter files of two different printings (Books S9–S10) whose shared chapter 20 is a different chapter in each, and NO chapter-26 file exists in either split — the English twin's chapter 26 is Algebraic fractions; the hole is carried as a set gap, not as read; (2) the 2018 Sinhala Grade 9 teacher guide is filed twice (two scans of one work) and is placed once (Book T2), and the file held as the Grade 7 2008 manual's "Tamil" copy is byte-identical to the English manual (same git blob SHA) and is cited once, as the English manual (Book T10); (3) the year-less Tamil Grade 4 and Grade 5 guides are proved by byte size to be NIE's 2015 editions, not the far larger 2019/2020 scans NIE also lists (Books T39–T40); (4) the 1976 Grade 12 Mathematics Part I scan (Book S23) ends with some six pages of an unrelated German textbook's verb tables (PDF pp. 271–276) — its mathematics runs to PDF p. 270 and its last chapters were sampled, not contents-verified; (5) the Grade 1 Sinhala 2026 guide is in hand as a Term 1 volume only (Book T33) — its Terms 2–3 are stocked nowhere — and the Grade 1 Tamil 2016 guide (Book T31) is marked DRAFT on its cover and is placed as filed; (6) the Grade 11 guide's Term 2 table labels one lesson (competency 23.6, inside the geometry cluster) "Central limit theorem" — recorded as printed in Book T19's entry; no row in this course rests on that label; (7) Grade 10 Mathematics Part I (English) and Grade 7 Mathematics Part I (Tamil, 2007–2015 edition) are NOT in hand in any filing — library gaps, not unread books; their twins in the other medium are held and read (Books S17 and S13/S14). The placements' named exclusions — revision exercises, answers, glossaries, assessment apparatus, and the guides' specimen lesson plans and school-based assessment instruments beyond their sequences — are stocked nowhere. Sri Lanka's class for Maths is **COURSES-CLOSED** — every in-hand book (94/94 files) has been read and placed, on the in-hand definition.

Into this lesson the placements stock 50 items (21 from pupil books, 29 teacher evidence) from Book S1, Book S3, Book S5, Book S6, Book S7, Book S9, Book S10, Book S11, Book S13, Book S15, Book T1, Book T2, Book T3, Book T4, Book T5, Book T6, Book T8, Book T9, Book T10, Book T11, Book T12, Book T13, Book T14, Book T15, Book T16, Book T39, Book T40.

## Andorra added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Andorra's whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs — **4 documents, all of them official Escola Andorrana curriculum programmes published by decree in the Butlletí Oficial del Principat d'Andorra (BOPA); 0 pupil (student) books** — and placed 679 curriculum-requirement items. A programme states what is REQUIRED to be taught; it teaches nothing itself. Every Andorra item in this section is therefore TEACHER EVIDENCE — a curriculum requirement, worded as a requirement — cited as the evidence kind it is; Andorra is counted in NO lesson's taught-in figure, on the standing Ghana evidence-only precedent, until Andorran pupil books are in hand and read. Set correction, as the placements prove it: the expansion map recorded 3 books in hand; the true set is 4 — the grades 1–6 programme (Decret 376/2021, Annex VII) is held in the GitHub repo but sits in no staging books.json, which is why the map missed it. All four programmes are Andorra's OWN (Govern d'Andorra, in Catalan); the Spanish and French systems also taught in Andorra (Spain's Anaya and Innovamat manuals; France's Le Livre Scolaire) are list-only names on official school book lists — no file is held, so they are cited nowhere in this course and credited to no country — and the Escola Andorrana itself has no commercial pupil-textbook series, so programmes are all that exist to hold. The placements' counting grain is one item per distinct stated requirement — each specific competency, and each distinct entry in the programmes' learning-resource tables, tagged by cycle where the table splits by cycle — and a procedure entry that merely restates a concept entry is folded into it, not double-counted. The placements' named exclusions — the decree preambles, the introductions and competency rationale, the didactic orientations, the assessment criteria and end-of-cycle expectations, the synthesis tables (verified verbatim repeats of the competency sections), the attitudes/values lists, Book 1's glossary and opening summary table, and the Council of Europe cross-references — are stocked nowhere. Book 3's synthesis table words its C2 end-of-cycle expectation as modelling "situacions cientificotècniques" (scientific-technical) inside the social-sciences programme — a copy-over slip in the decree as published; Book 3's competency section itself is consistently social-sciences, and the slip is copied into no row below. Book 2 states its cone/polyhedron sections twice (forms table and models table, printed pp. 32–33) — counted once in the placements and stocked once. Printed page = PDF page in all four books (Books 2–4 carry the BOPA folio; Book 1's Portal PDF is paginated at the foot the same way). Into this lesson the placements stock 8 teacher-evidence items from Book 1, Book 2.

**Book 1:** *Programa de matemàtiques de primera ensenyança* (curriculum programme, Grades 1–6) — Andorra, Govern d'Andorra, Escola Andorrana; Decret 376/2021, Annex VII; ISBN 978-99920-0-934-5; 688,455 bytes, 49 PDF pages; printed page = PDF page. TEACHER EVIDENCE ONLY — an official curriculum programme: it states what is required to be taught and teaches nothing itself.

**Book 2:** *Programa de matemàtiques de segona ensenyança* (curriculum programme, Grades 7–10) — Andorra, Govern d'Andorra, Escola Andorrana; Decret 355/2023 (BOPA Núm. 91, 19-7-2023); ISBN 978-99920-0-847-2; 1,808,872 bytes, 56 PDF pages; printed page = PDF page (the BOPA folio). TEACHER EVIDENCE ONLY — an official curriculum programme: it states what is required to be taught and teaches nothing itself.

## Angola added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Angola's whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs — **6 books, all pupil textbooks: Matemática, Manuais da 1.ª–6.ª Classe (Ministério da Educação — MED, Angola; updated editions of 2021, in Portuguese). There is no teacher guide in the Angola Maths set in hand, so every Angola row in this course is a pupil-textbook row — there are no teacher-evidence rows, and Angola counts in every lesson it stocks** — and placed 229 teaching items. The expansion map's “6 student + 0 teacher = 6” is correct: download-lists/angola.md, the phase3-staging checklist, textbooks.tsv, phase3-push-state.json (“pushed”) and the staging books.json files all give the same six distinct pupil textbooks, and nothing else. None of the six PDFs was on local disk (pusher-secured only), so all six were fetched fresh for the read from the books' recorded second official source, the ministry's Xilonga host, byte-exact against the records. One set nuance: the portal's 32-manual set contains no 2.ª Classe Matemática at all — the 2.ª book in hand (Book 2) exists only as an unlinked Xilonga file (MAT02-LR.pdf) found by pattern probe on 2026-10-23. A Guia Prático Matemática 6ª exists only as an exact-link reader copy and is NOT in hand — cited nowhere; if one is ever secured it enters as teacher evidence only. The Book 2 and Book 4 PDFs carry a massively duplicated text layer (the same text overprinted many times per page) — a production flaw of the files; the content is fully legible and both books were read in full from it. Twelve book errors are recorded in the placements; they are carried in the book entries below and taught corrected. The placements' named exclusions — the apresentação (ministerial foreword letter) and anthem/flag/civic front matter, the bibliografia pages, pure colouring, tracing and copying practice with no new concept, the editorial production notes printed inside the books, and exercise sets as such (exercises practise the item under which they sit) — are stocked nowhere. Printed-page bases: Books 1 and 3 — printed page = PDF page − 1; Books 2, 4, 5 and 6 — printed page = PDF page. Into this lesson the placements stock 3 items (all from pupil textbooks) from Book 3, Book 4 and Book 5.

**Book 3:** *Matemática — Manual da 3.ª Classe* (pupil textbook, Grade 3) — Angola, Ministério da Educação (MED), Progresso Editora, 2021, 1.ª Edição, ISBN 978-989-762-270-0; 1,798,728 bytes, 85 PDF pp. Pages cited are the book's printed pages (printed page = PDF page − 1). Book errors, taught corrected: printed p. 21 repeats the minuend misnomer “diminuendo ou aditivo” (teach: minuend); printed p. 36's decimal-multiplication worked example is internally inconsistent — it states 4,15 × 2,3, lays out 3,15 and prints 7,245 (correct: 4,15 × 2,3 = 9,545); the exercise under “Noção de circunferência” at printed p. 44 repeats the parallel-lines exercise verbatim (a copy-paste error in the book); printed p. 62 prints the false line “20 m = 200 cm” (correct: 20 m = 2 000 cm); printed p. 64's formula under decâmetro repeats “1 hm = 100 m” (correct: 1 dam = 10 m).

**Book 4:** *Matemática — Manual da 4.ª Classe* (pupil textbook, Grade 4) — Angola, Ministério da Educação (MED), Mayamba Editora, 2021, 1.ª Edição, ISBN 978-989-761-296-1; 1,983,954 bytes, 112 PDF pp. Pages cited are the book's printed pages (printed page = PDF page). Book error, taught corrected: printed p. 43 claims division “is only possible if the dividend is a multiple of the divisor” (exact division only) — contradicted by Book 3's own division-with-remainder teaching; the course teaches division with remainder. Editorial production notes are printed as page content on printed pp. 17, 18, 21, 24, 25, 28, 30 and 45 — production matter, cited nowhere; the Tema 3 heading is misprinted inside the book (at printed p. 102); PDF pp. 106–112 are blank/back matter apart from the bibliografia (printed p. 107), cited nowhere. The PDF's text layer is massively duplicated (the same text overprinted many times per page) — a production flaw of the file; the content is fully legible and the book was read in full from it.

**Book 5:** *Matemática — Manual da 5.ª Classe* (pupil textbook, Grade 5) — Angola, Ministério da Educação (MED), Texto Editores, 2021, 2.ª Edição, ISBN 978-989-8884-84-8; 4,730,235 bytes, 128 PDF pp. Pages cited are the book's printed pages (printed page = PDF page). Book errors, taught corrected: printed p. 29's worked example 12 + 37 + 7 + 3 is printed as = 55 with a garbled regrouping (correct total: 59); printed p. 73's equivalence line prints 1/3 = 2/6 = 2/9 (the last term should be 3/9); printed p. 127's median worked example uses an ordered list containing 12, which is not in the exercise's data, and drops one 10 (the median is taught from the data as printed in the exercise: ordered 6, 7, 9, 10, 10, 10, 11, 13, 14 → median 10).

## Bahamas added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read the Bahamas' whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs — **4 documents, all of them Bahamas Ministry of Education curriculum or examination-syllabus documents; 0 pupil (student) textbooks and 0 teacher guides** — and placed 496 requirement items. A curriculum states what is REQUIRED to be taught; it teaches nothing itself. Every Bahamas item in this section is therefore CURRICULUM EVIDENCE — a statement of what the Bahamas requires, worded as a requirement — cited as the evidence kind it is (Book 3's items are examination-SYLLABUS evidence, on the same standing); the Bahamas is counted in NO lesson's taught-in figure, on the standing Ghana/Barbados evidence-only precedent, until a Bahamas pupil book is in hand and read. Set correction, as the placements prove it: the expansion map recorded the in-hand set as “4 student + 0 teacher” — the count of 4 is right, the kind is wrong; all four documents are ministry requirement documents (kind = curriculum in both the staging books.json files and textbooks.tsv). The file the records title *BJC Mathematics Curriculum, Grades 7–9* is actually the *Bahamas Junior Certificate 044 Mathematics Syllabus 2003* — a 10-page scanned examination syllabus with no text layer, read visually page by page — and the held Senior High file is the July 2019 revision of the Grades 10–12 guidelines; both are placed as what they are. No pupil textbook or teacher guide is in hand: the pupil books Bahamas schools use are commercial (Hodder *Bahamas Primary Mathematics* Books 1–6 — existence verified in the records, no file held; Books 1–2 name Silver Burdett Ginn Mathematics and Harcourt Math in classroom use, and Book 4's bibliography names STP Caribbean Mathematics, Mathematics: A Complete Course, Fundamental Mathematics for the Caribbean and Nelson Caribbean Mathematics — named only, none held), and the one school-compiled pupil text with an exact link in the records (Akhepran International Academy, *Mathematics BJC Textbook — Grade 7*, 2023) was never downloaded and sits in no record set, so it is not part of this read and is cited nowhere. The placements' counting grain is one item per distinct numbered objective in each guidelines book's Scope of Work body — the Content column read with every objective — Book 3 at one item per numbered topic row of its Topics table and Book 4 at one item per CONCEPTS/TOPICS block; an objective restated in full on a continuation page is counted once, at its first full statement, and the two requirements the documents restate inside a second strand (Book 1 Grade 2 estimation, item 69; Book 2 Grade 5 rounding of decimals, item 256) are stocked as the placements place them, each row naming the restatement. The placements' named exclusions — Benchmarks, Scope-and-Sequence charts, pacing guides and Essential Questions in Books 1–2; the Problems of the Day sets, Materials Lists, assessment rubrics, the Children's Literature list and References (record items R1–R7 and R9–R11); Book 3's examination administration, its 19 general aims and its erratum sheet (R8); Book 4's Scope and Sequence charts, Glossary and Technology Integration pages — are stocked nowhere. Book errors found in the read are carried corrected on the rows they touch. Into this lesson the placements stock 11 requirement-evidence items from Book 2, Book 3 and Book 4.

**Book 2:** *Primary School Mathematics Curriculum Guidelines, Grades 4–6* (curriculum guidelines) — Bahamas, Ministry of Education, Department of Education, September 2010; 5,278,639 bytes, 328 PDF pages; Scope of Work printed page = PDF page − 54. CURRICULUM EVIDENCE ONLY — a curriculum states what is required to be taught; it teaches nothing itself.

**Book 3:** *Bahamas Junior Certificate 044 Mathematics Syllabus 2003* (examination syllabus) — Bahamas, Ministry of Education, Youth & Sports, Testing & Evaluation Section; 2,351,047 bytes, 10 PDF pages (a scanned-image PDF with no text layer, read visually page by page); printed page = PDF page − 1. The library records title this file “BJC Mathematics Curriculum, Grades 7–9” — the held file is the 2003 examination syllabus, and it is placed as what it is (set correction, stated here; the records themselves are not edited — merges never edit the records). The Bahamas' class for Maths is PARTIAL (evidence-only): all 4 in-hand documents are read and stocked, but no pupil textbook is in hand; it advances only when a pupil book file — a Hodder *Bahamas Primary Mathematics* volume, or the Akhepran Grade 7 BJC textbook whose exact link already sits in the download list — lands in hand and is read.

## Australia added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Australia's whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs — **9 books, all of them student textbooks** (Years 8–10: eight Cambridge volumes — the Essential Mathematics Australian Curriculum, Victorian Curriculum and CambridgeMATHS NSW lines, plus the GOLD support edition and the CORE pathway volume — and one Jacaranda volume, Maths Quest 10+10A) — and placed 844 teaching items. Australia has no government textbook series (ACARA writes the Australian Curriculum but publishes and prescribes no textbooks); the set is the school-used commercial series the country records name, and every book is cited as such. All 9 files are distinct works (distinct ISBNs, extents and contents, verified per book on the read); the publisher-line relations are stated in the placements: Book A1 (GOLD) is the support edition of Book A2's Year 8 material, Book A3 is the same line in its Victorian ©2024 edition, Books A4 and A6 are the NSW syllabus editions, and Book A9 is Book A8's CORE pathway companion. In the sections below the books are cited as Books A1–A9. Australia COUNTS in this lesson's taught-in figure: every item is pupil-textbook teaching. The read's four flagged claims are carried exactly: (F1) Book A3's contents lists its Chapter 11, Algorithmic thinking, as “(online only)”, but the file in hand contains the full chapter, bound after the Answers (printed pp. 926–941) — it is read and placed, and it founds this course's algorithmic-thinking lesson; (F2) Book A7's contents lists a Chapter 18, Algorithmic thinking, with no page numbers, and the chapter is NOT in the file (searched end to end; the book closes with its Glossary, printed p. 1106, and Index, printed p. 1117) — recorded as absent, not read, and no item rests on it; (F3) Book A9's running head at printed p. 644 prints section 10E's code as “0E” — the book's own production error; the placement cites 10E, as the section's heading and contents give it; (F4) only the 9 Maths books are this read's set — the country records' wider Australia picture (12 staged books across subjects, plus filled-by-exact-link titles) is not merged into this count, and in particular the two senior Maths titles that exist only as exact-link records (Cambridge Senior Specialist Mathematics, QCE Units 3&4, 2e, and Cambridge Senior Mathematical Methods, QCE Units 3&4, 2e) are NOT in hand and were NOT read. The placements' named exclusions — front matter and “how to use this book” guides; every chapter's pre-tests, progress quizzes, unlettered applications / problem-solving / modelling / investigation spreads, puzzles and games, chapter summaries, checklists and reviews, semester reviews, glossaries, indexes and answer sections — are apparatus/assessment, teach no new content, and are stocked nowhere. The set is Years 8–10 secondary only, so the primary lessons (counting, place value, first steps) receive no Australia item — that is the set's shape, not a reading gap. Australia's class for Maths is **COURSES-CLOSED** — every in-hand book (9/9) has been read and placed; the two senior QCE exact-link titles do not block the in-hand class (if exact-link titles are ever ruled in-scope for the courses reckoning, Australia drops to PARTIAL until they are grabbed and read). Into this lesson the placements stock 14 items from Book A1, Book A2, Book A3, Book A4, Book A5, Book A6, Book A8, Book A9.

**Book A1:** *Cambridge Mathematics 8 (CambridgeMaths GOLD)* — Australia, Greenwood et al., Cambridge University Press, ©2012, ISBN 978-1-107-64383-3; 62,774,078 bytes, 554 PDF pp. The GOLD support edition of the Essential Mathematics Year 8 line, developed for students who require additional support in mathematics; a distinct work from Book A2 (different ISBN, extent and arrangement), not a duplicate file. Pages cited are printed pages (printed = PDF − 14).

**Book A2:** *Essential Mathematics for the Australian Curriculum Year 8, Third Edition* — Australia, Greenwood et al., Cambridge University Press, ©2019, ISBN 978-1-108-77281-5; 35,833,730 bytes, 846 PDF pp. The main Year 8 Australian Curriculum volume of the Essential Mathematics line (Book A1 is its Gold support sibling). Pages cited are printed pages (printed = PDF − 18).

**Book A3:** *Essential Mathematics for the Victorian Curriculum Year 8, Third Edition* — Australia, Greenwood et al., Cambridge University Press, ©2024, ISBN 978-1-009-48075-8; 24,871,876 bytes, 955 PDF pp. The Victorian Curriculum state edition of the Essential Mathematics Year 8 line — a distinct work from Book A2. Pages cited are printed pages (printed = PDF − 20). Its contents lists Chapter 11, Algorithmic thinking, as “(online only)”, but the full chapter IS bound into this file, after the Answers (printed pp. 926–941), and is read and placed (flag F1).

**Book A4:** *CambridgeMATHS NSW Stage 4 (Year 8), Second Edition* — Australia, Palmer, McDaid, Greenwood et al., Cambridge University Press, ©2018, ISBN 978-1-108-46627-1; 27,138,098 bytes, 680 PDF pp. The NSW syllabus edition for Stage 4 Year 8. Pages cited are printed pages (printed = PDF − 16). It has no probability chapter — its statistics chapter is data-only; that is the volume's real shape (its contents were read in full), not a missing part of the file.

**Book A5:** *Essential Mathematics for the Australian Curriculum Year 9, Third Edition* — Australia, Greenwood, Woolley, Goodman et al., Cambridge University Press, ©2020, ISBN 978-1-108-77288-4; 148,251,307 bytes, 848 PDF pp. Year 9 volume of the same Essential Mathematics AC line as Book A2. Pages cited are printed pages (printed = PDF − 18).

**Book A6:** *CambridgeMATHS NSW Stage 5 (Year 9), Second Edition* — Australia, Palmer, Greenwood, Woolley et al., Cambridge University Press, ©2019, ISBN 978-1-108-46816-9; 114,064,205 bytes, 743 PDF pp. The NSW Stage 5 (pathways 5.1/5.2/5.3 in one volume) Year 9 book — the NSW counterpart of Book A5. Pages cited are printed pages (printed = PDF − 18). Its Chapter 5 contents columns interleave in extraction; its section folios were each verified individually on the read.

Pages cited are printed pages (printed = PDF − 18).

Pages cited are printed pages (printed = PDF − 14). ## Brazil added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Brazil's whole in-hand Maths set from the PDF — **1 book, a pupil textbook: *Matemática — Livro Didático Público — Ensino Médio* (SEED-PR), the Paraná state public textbook for Ensino Médio (Grades 10–12), in Portuguese. There is no teacher guide in the Brazil Maths set in hand, so every Brazil row in this course is a pupil-textbook row — there are no teacher-evidence rows, and Brazil counts in every lesson it stocks** — and placed 128 teaching items. The expansion map's “1 student + 0 teacher = 1” is correct: books.json (grade-10/gov/brazil) and textbooks.tsv each carry exactly one Brazil Mathematics entry, and phase3-push-state.json lists the file as pushed. The staging folder for the book is empty, so there was no local copy to read, and the authenticated GitHub asset route failed in this pass (the mid-size-books release's asset listing repeatedly disconnected through the proxy, and the asset's download URL answered 404) — the GitHub copy's existence and size were therefore NOT verified here, and a library-side check of that asset is owed (a route failure, not a negative finding). The book was instead read from its own recorded source_url — the Wayback capture banked by the 2026-10-22 CDX prep (capture 20240421222141) — which landed at exactly the prep's expected 6,379,928 B, with 240 pages and the correct cover and catalogue block; the book read is certainly this book. Printed page = PDF page throughout. Four book errors found in the read (placements E1–E4) are carried corrected on the rows they touch — in the sequences-and-series (E1), ratio-and-proportion (E2), coordinate-geometry (E3), barycentres (E4) and loci (E4) lessons; two further misprints are recorded, not taught (R5: the risk exponent typeset once as 0.2525 for 0.2528, printed p. 58 — noted on the item 28 row in the logarithms-and-exponentials lesson; R6: glucose printed “C6A12O6”, printed p. 35, a chemistry misprint), and the Aristarchus bound passage (printed p. 169, record item R7) could not be confirmed either way as extracted and is cited nowhere. The placements' named exclusions — the four section introductions and the general presentation (framing and history), the context essays and debates that carry no new maths, the biographical and historical passages except where the passage itself teaches a method, the “Obras Consultadas” reference lists after every chapter, and the blank “Anotações” pages — are stocked nowhere. Into this lesson the placements stock 2 items (all from the pupil textbook) from Book 1.

**Book 1:** *Matemática — Livro Didático Público — Ensino Médio* (pupil textbook, Ensino Médio — Grades 10–12) — Brazil, Secretaria de Estado da Educação do Paraná (SEED-PR), Curitiba, 2nd edition 2006, ISBN 85-85380-39-X; 6,379,928 bytes, 240 PDF pp., MD5 e3dac71a6d075dd142282376da6fa785. Pages cited are the book's printed pages (printed page = PDF page). Read from the banked Wayback capture of the book's recorded source_url at the exact recorded size; the GitHub release-asset copy could not be verified in this pass (route failure — a library-side asset check is owed). The catalogue block's “p. 216” is the 1st-edition pagination and does not match this file's 240 pages (record item R1); all citations use the actual printed folios. Folha 6's chapter heading (printed p. 77) adds “Corrupção & Política –” to the contents-list title (record item R2) — the same chapter. Folhas 9 and 10 are filed by the book inside its Funções section although their content is trigonometry (record item R3); the placements follow the content.

| Complementary angles (sum 90°), supplementary angles (sum 180°) and adjacent angles | Cabo Verde — *Matemática 7º ano — Manual do(a) aluno(a)* (student manual), printed pp. 85–86 |
| Angles with parallel sides are equal or supplementary | Cabo Verde — *Matemática 7º ano — Manual do(a) aluno(a)* (student manual), printed p. 86 |
| Angles of a triangle: sum of the interior angles is 180°; an exterior angle equals the sum of the two non-adjacent interior angles | Cabo Verde — *Matemática 7º ano — Manual do(a) aluno(a)* (student manual), printed pp. 94–95 |
| Sum of the interior angles of a polygon, built up in the consolidation table from triangles (180° per triangle), i.e. (n − 2) × 180° | Cabo Verde — *Matemática 7º ano — Manual do(a) aluno(a)* (student manual), printed pp. 110–111 |

## Canada added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Canada's whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs — **2 works in 4 files: *Math First Peoples — Teacher Resource Guide, Elementary & Secondary* (FNESC/FNSA, ©2020), 273 pp. — the one book in Canada's classified in-hand Maths set (0 student + 1 teacher) — and the borderline pupil work *I Like Math!* (Government of Nunavut, 2017), one Level 8 guided-reading fiction book held in three language editions of 11 pp. each and filed in every record under the Inuktut Ilinniaqta Early Books label, not under Maths** — and placed 117 items (112 teacher-guide evidence items from the guide, items 1–112, plus 5 pupil items from *I Like Math!*, items S1–S5). Every item from the guide is TEACHER-GUIDE EVIDENCE: the guide directs teachers how to teach BC mathematics through First Peoples contexts, with mini-units, activities and blackline masters, and states BC curriculum connections in its tables; it expressly does not explain the mathematics or the curriculum in detail (its printed p. 35) and presumes a trained teacher. No pupil Maths textbook is in hand for Canada: Canada has no national textbook series (education is provincial/territorial), and the five provincial student textbooks in the records (Nelson *Principles of Mathematics 10*; McGraw-Hill Ryerson *Principles of Mathematics 9*; Pearson *Math Makes Sense 3, 7, 8*) stand filled-by-exact-link only — never staged, never secured, not read here and cited nowhere (a sixth, *Nelson Mathematics 8*, is a candidate only, its imprint never read). Canada is therefore cited as EVIDENCE ONLY, on the standing Ghana evidence-only precedent: Canada is added to this lesson's countries line and named in its rows, but counted in NO lesson's taught-in figure. The *I Like Math!* items are stocked as pupil evidence from that work exactly as the placements label them — 3 carry taught content (S1, S2, S4) and 2 are context/named-only (S3, S5) — and are likewise counted in no taught-in figure; whether the Nunavut early-books titles count as pupil Maths at all is a record-side question for the parent, reported here, not ruled on. The guide's Water-Keepers items (98–105) rest on the guide's printed teaching alone: the separate FNESC Water Keepers digital resource the guide points to (printed p. 224) is in no record as in hand and was not read. The guide is the 2020 revision of the 2011 *Math First Peoples* resource; the 2011 predecessor located on a Padlet host is not a separate in-hand book and is cited nowhere. The placements' named exclusions — the guide's introduction, goals/rationale and teacher-reflection material; First Peoples Principles of Learning, story protocols and respectful-practice rules; the resource-design rubric; community-visit apparatus beyond the Interview Guide's six content categories; unit-organization front matter; the cultural/historical context narratives and stories inside the mini-units (context-setting only; no story text is reproduced anywhere in the placements or here); BLM worksheets that merely repeat an itemised activity; formative/summative assessment prompts, guiding questions, video and website lists and the Additional Resources bibliographies; and the *I Like Math!* front/back matter — teach no new mathematics and are stocked nowhere. The 5 book errors found in the read (placements E1–E5) are carried corrected on the rows they touch: E1 (sphere-volume worked example, printed p. 123 — a unit slip and a garbled intermediate line inside a volume calculation; the final answer, 500 cm³, is correct) on item 56; E2 (BLM 6 conversion sheet, printed p. 185 — the percent-to-decimal rule states the decimal-point direction backwards; both printed examples are correct) on item 83; E3 (Moose Tracks answer key, printed p. 181 — mixes “less than or equal to 20” with m < 20 and marks the boundary value inconsistently) on item 71; E4 (Circle Unit Answer Key, printed p. 211 — says “smoke hole” for “fire circle”; the project instructions at printed p. 204 state it correctly) on item 92; E5 (Weaving BLM list, printed p. 55 — numbers both Keep or Toss sheets BLM 16; they are BLM 15 and BLM 16) is a list typo affecting no item's content. Printed page = PDF page in the guide throughout; *I Like Math!* citations are its printed story folios. Canada's class for Maths is **PARTIAL (evidence-only)**. Into this lesson the placements stock 2 items (items 14 and 44).

**Book 1:** *Math First Peoples — Teacher Resource Guide, Elementary & Secondary* (teacher resource guide) — Canada, First Nations Education Steering Committee (FNESC) and First Nations Schools Association (FNSA), ©2020; 32,264,979 bytes (byte-exact against the record), 273 PDF pages. Printed page = PDF page throughout. TEACHER-GUIDE EVIDENCE ONLY — the guide directs teachers how to teach BC mathematics through First Peoples contexts and states curriculum connections; it expressly does not explain the mathematics in detail (printed p. 35); it is not a pupil textbook.

| Lesson part | Where it comes from |
|---|---|
| Canoe-box kerf angles: ends and outer kerfs parallel, middle kerf a reflection, all obtuse angles equal, all acute angles equal, obtuse + acute supplementary (sum 180°) | Canada — *Math First Peoples — Teacher Resource Guide* (FNESC/FNSA, 2020) (teacher guide) — teacher-guide evidence, printed p. 47 |
| Calculating stave angles for a drum frame: 360 ÷ (number of staves × number of sides cut) = angle of cut | Canada — *Math First Peoples — Teacher Resource Guide* (FNESC/FNSA, 2020) (teacher guide) — teacher-guide evidence, printed p. 108 |

## Costa Rica added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Costa Rica's whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs — **25 books, all of them student books: the five CONED distance-secondary antologías *Antología de Matemáticas*, Niveles 7–11 (UNED/CONED — Nivel Sétimo 2017, Nivel Noveno 2018, Nivel 11 2019; Nivel Décimo by MSc. Jorge Díaz Porras) and the ITCR Proyecto EVEPRIM primary books for Grades 3 and 6 (10 textos + 10 folletos de prácticas across Relaciones y Álgebra, Números, Geometría, Medidas, Estadística and Probabilidad); 0 teacher guides for Costa Rica Maths exist in hand** — and placed 229 teaching items. **SET CORRECTION: the expansion map's count of 17 student is WRONG** — 17 is exactly the number of Costa Rica Mathematics rows in textbooks.tsv (10 EVEPRIM textos + only 2 of the 10 EVEPRIM folletos + 5 CONED antologías); the TSV was never updated for 8 folletos staged on 2026-10-25, and the staging books.json files and phase3-push-state.json agree on the true set of 25 (the folletos count student-side, per the map's own stated basis). All 25 were grabbed, verified (byte size + MD5 against books.json; the two Relaciones y Álgebra textos, which have no books.json entry, verified by size + title page as the main-repo files) and read in full — no partial excerpts, no duplicates (the SharePoint folder copies of the six texto books matched the staged sizes byte-for-byte, so they are the same files, cited once) and no unopenable files. The three Grade 3 textos were read in their November 2026 editions, the in-hand editions per books.json; the GitHub release mid-size-books-3 still holds the superseded October editions of those three textos (a record-level flag, reported, not fixed — same books, older exports; only the November editions are cited here). Two chapter promises in the CONED books are NOT teaching and place no item anywhere: CONED 7's Capítulo 4 is titled Estadística y Probabilidad but teaches statistics only (probability appears only in its introductory discussion questions, pp. 209–214), and CONED 10's Capítulo 1 objectives promise plane transformations that no Tema in the chapter teaches (only a recall of Grade 8 homothety). The placements' named exclusions — front matter, indices, bibliographies and answer keys (soluciones); the EVEPRIM answer-key chapters and the review chapters that re-teach already-placed sections; the CONED chapter motto/introduction pages and “desafío matemático” pages; and the videos and web interactives the EVEPRIM books link to — are apparatus and are stocked nowhere. The 5 CONED book-error groups found in the read — CONED 7: the angle table (p. 106) labels a 360° angle “CONVEXO”, the inverse-proportionality example (pp. 201–203) states v·t = 247.5 for a product of 330 and “very close to 3 hours” at 78 km/h for ≈4.23 h with garbled fraction work, the triangle inequality (p. 125) omits “any two” sides, the median answer (p. 238) gives 68 for a median of 67, the worked powers page (p. 64) misaligns its results, and a tiling exercise (p. 35) reads “largo por largo”; CONED 8: the table of contents numbers the equations Tema “Tema 4” while the body heads it “Tema 5” (numbering slip only); CONED 9: the SI prefix table (p. 74) gives exa the symbol “R” (exa is E), the radian-to-degree rule (p. 117) is printed inverted/garbled though its worked examples are correct, and the contents duplicates “Función Cuadrática” for Capítulo 3 Temas 1 and 4 and mislabels Capítulo 4 as “Capítulo III”; CONED 10: the falling-body model (p. 373) prints “12” for ½ (the worked values use ½ correctly), the key (p. 42) duplicates “tangentes” where “secantes” is meant, p. 14 asks for the area of a “circunferencia” (the circle), and the pentagon solution (p. 86) drops its degree symbols; CONED 11: the logarithm table (p. 118) misaligns its examples (it prints log₅ 5 = 2, which belongs to log₅ 25), the wound-model solution (p. 172) shows an unsigned division display before the correct result, and “invectiva” (p. 121) is a typo for inyectiva — are carried corrected on the rows they touch. One further note is carried as the placements carry it: the Medidas G3 answer key's building-height estimate reads 45 dam in the October copy and “4 dam” in the November extraction — treated as a re-export/extraction artefact in the key, not teaching content, and cited nowhere. Costa Rica's books are pupil books, so Costa Rica is COUNTED: it joins this lesson's acceptance count wherever the placements stock an item. CONED printed page = PDF page throughout; EVEPRIM pages are cited by PDF page with the book's section numbers (two exceptions print folios: the EVEPRIM 6 Probabilidad texto's printed folio = PDF page − 6 and the EVEPRIM 3 Medidas texto's = PDF page − 7; citations give PDF pages). Into this lesson the placements stock 6 items (items 12, 13, 133, 209, 210 and 211).

**Book 1:** *Antología de Matemáticas — Nivel Sétimo* (student antología) — Costa Rica, Universidad Estatal a Distancia (UNED) / Colegio Nacional de Educación a Distancia (CONED), 2017; 6,973,434 bytes, 242 PDF pp. Printed page = PDF page throughout. Pupil book (distance-secondary antología).
